# Reminders: work parked until the right moment

## Standing rules

The owner's two most important rules (2026-10-24). They never close; the
hooks print them first, at every session start and before every device run
or test-image build.

- **Iterate, don't imitate.** What Dell, Windows Mobile, Linux or any 2005 design does is a finding, never the answer or a ceiling. For every inherited value or behaviour: is it a hardware limit or a choice? If a choice, find the better ways (our own software, the device's parts working together), measure them, and beat the original; keep the old way only where a measurement says it wins. **Be clever:** we have today's technology and software, AI included, that Dell didn't have in 2005: use it to make everything as good, as fast and as efficient as it can possibly be. **Modernize** everything that can be modernized, while keeping the neo-retro look.
- **Nothing is impossible or a limit without proof.** Never tell the owner something can't be done, or call a setting a hardware limit, until every idea and every "did we do it wrong?" check is ruled out by a measurement. Divide 1 was called unworkable, and it now runs 2.17x Windows Mobile's speed at 108.33 MHz: giving up early leaves performance on the table.

## The reminders

**Why this exists (the owner, 2026-10-24):** plans were made and then
dropped because nothing brought them back at the right time (12 found on
2026-10-24: ROADMAP step 3's give-up audit table). Anything parked, whether
the owner raised it or a session found it, goes on this list **in the same
reply that parks it**, with a trigger saying when it must come back up.

**How it comes back up (automatic, `.claude/settings.json`):**
- **Every session start** prints the open reminders (`tools/remind/remind.py
  session`).
- **Before a device run or a test-image build** (`cargo xtask load`,
  `codegen-variant`) the reminders whose trigger is `device-run`, or names
  that image (`image:rp-gpubench`), are printed: the moment to fold them
  into the run.
- **`cargo xtask ci`** checks this table's format and prints the open count.

**The rules:**
- **Trigger** is one or more of, separated by `;`: `session` (every session
  start), `device-run` (any device run), `image:<name>` (a run or build of
  that image), `after:<what>` (when that lands; shown at session start),
  `date:YYYY-MM-DD` (shown from that day). Every trigger is also shown at
  session start, so nothing waits on its trigger alone.
- **Status** is `open`, or `done <commit or run>`, or `dropped <the owner's
  reason>` (only the owner drops one). Rows are never deleted.
- Every summary to the owner names the reminders that came due.
- The owner can add, edit or drop rows himself.

| R20 | C's tearing design (ADR-0034 P1 = C): the core's tile write order measured, the render started behind the scan line, frames longer than a refresh. **GC2BZ ran 2026-10-27 (gpubench-0152): a top-down order (rows, interleaved) begun at line 320 tear-free in 40 of 40 at both clocks, the 19.6 ms frame too; the rule: start at line >= 640 - T/0.0275 (T the render in ms), any render up to ~35 ms fits; checker (the shipped divide-1 cure order) and reversed tear in every render (2700g.md "GC2BZ on the X51v"). The design (start line per frame, order) is the owner's to rule.** **Ruled by the owner 2026-10-28, for the shell and apps: C first, A as the fallback.** The scheduler picks each frame's start line from the last frame's time (`>= 640 - T / 0.0275`); checker-order frames and very long frames (past ~35 ms) take A's copy path; **added by the owner: the scheduler must also handle a frame that suddenly takes much longer than the last one** (a spike, not only steadily long frames), so a late frame never overtakes the scan. **The games path (the half-resolution pair, R78 item 2) is held** until GC2CA shows whether it pays and the owner has judged its picture with his own eye. Open: the design (ADR-0034's P1 recorded as C with A's fallback) and the build. **Built and tested 2026-10-29 (`rp_mbx3d_render::scan`, GC2CZ on the X51v: 120 of 120 frames begun behind the scan tear-free at WM clock and 74.75 / 1 through 2x spikes, A on the first frame and after each spike); left: wiring into the graphics service's frame loop, the games path (held)** **The fallback ruled by the owner 2026-10-29 ("Option 1", ADR-0034 under rule 5): Copy-plan frames render into screen 1, flip in the blanking, area copied back to screen 0; first a soak of 3D targets at screen 1's place at 149.50 / 2 with the display on screen 0; until it passes, those frames start at line 640 on screen 0; the scheduler takes the measured line period (R111). Build order: the soak run (rp-gpubench, emulator-checked, the owner's go), then the service wiring with host tests (Behind, Copy-before-proof, Copy-after-proof, spikes), then the device run of the service (fold R110's copy_stalls log in).** **The soak built 2026-10-29 (GC2DE, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_HALF_R20`, 2700g.md "R20's option 1: the screen-1 soak built"): 1,200 renders into screen 1's place at 149.50 / 2 checked word for word with screen 0 shown, each screen's render cost, the 2D copy back, and the blanking start against option 1 frame by frame; emulator-checked; waits for the owner's go.** **The service wiring built 2026-10-29 (`services/graphics`, `rp_mbx3d_render::scan`): each scene frame planned from its recent render times on the display's measured scan, picked by `DISPPLL` (60 Hz: 25,687 ns, R111; Windows Mobile's 56 Hz: 27,460 ns; any other clock: never behind the scan). Frames the scan can lead start on screen 0 at a line inside the window of their band of rows (`Schedule::window`: the scene's own rows, not the whole screen's: a scene low on the screen would tear at the whole screen's line), their lists written before the line so the start is only the start; the others go into screen 1 when `Graphics::allow_screen1_3d` is on (2D's frame held there is shown with them by the flip; screen 0 gets the area, and 2D's changes, once scan-out has left it, never blocking), else start on screen 0 once the scan has left the scene's area (for a full-screen scene that is the ruling's line 640; for a smaller one, its own rows' end: sooner, the same rule). Host tests 17 (6 new: behind and tear-free across the band, before and after the proof, a 30 ms spike then back behind, an unmeasured clock, a hang in screen 1), 6 mutants caught. Left: the device run of the service (an image driving a 3D scene through it, logging `starts()` and `copy_stalls()`, R110), after GC2DE's verdict decides whether option 1 is on.** **GC2DE ran 2026-10-29 (gpubench-0219): the soak clean (1,200 of 1,200 renders into screen 1 exact at 149.50 / 2, screen 0's DSIG never moved, no underrun); screen 1 renders cost the same as screen 0's except the textured frame (+15%); the copy back 3.1 ms whole screen; option 1 shows a fallback frame ~4 ms sooner (23.5 against 27.7 ms) and never torn, but frees the core for the next 3D frame ~12 ms later (40.1 against 27.7 ms) (2700g.md "GC2DE on the X51v"). Switching option 1 on (`allow_screen1_3d`) is the owner's call.** **The owner's direction 2026-10-29 ~2:30 PM, restated by Claude, CONFIRMED by the owner 2026-10-29 ("Confirmed, start fresh"); the back-to-back signal (arrival rate or a client hint) still his to pick: (1) fallback frames start on screen 0 once the scan has passed the scene's bottom row (line 640 for a full screen, sooner for a smaller scene), recomputed each frame, tests proving both, literal 640 if either fails; no 'most performant' claim until a device run shows a small scene sooner and tear-free; Claude proposes capping the start at the refresh's last line (640-644 for a full screen); a popup is 2D over the area, not a growing scene; a checkerboard render from 640 can still tear (counted). (2) option 1 on, chosen per frame: one-off fallback frames to screen 1 (time to screen 23.5 vs 27.7 ms), back-to-back ones the blanking start (throughput 27.7 vs 40.1 ms); the service sees no queue, so the owner picks the signal: arrival rate (a frame asked within one refresh of the last one shown = back-to-back) or a client hint; caveats carried (textured +15% into screen 1, copy 3.1/0.8 ms, C92). (3) a proof run at 149.50 / 2, four game frames, adaptive against both fixed policies, one-off and back-to-back: time to screen, next frame free, fps, tears (DSIG and screen 0 signature every render). (4) GC2DE's safety kept, every failed, hung or undrawn render counted (R96).** **Owed before the build (the owner asked 2026-10-29 whether rule 0 and REA were used: rule 0 only in part, REA not at all): (a) REA on Dell's swap and flip path (`ddi_2700g`, `d3d_2700g`, `libGLES_CL`): when the scan address latches, whether Dell waits on `DVLNUM` or a moveable `VEVENT0` line (`DVECTRL`), copy or flip, any third buffer; (b) the choices not yet weighed: the tile order per clock (the checkerboard is the divide-1 cure; at 149.50 / 2 a top-down order may make most fallback frames start behind the scan, so few need a fallback at all), an interrupt at a chosen line instead of polling, planning the next frame behind the scan right after the flip back (cutting option 1's 40 ms), the textured +15% (banks lead); (c) tests not yet run: the blanking start's tears measured by DSIG on a checkerboard render, the service itself on the device (R110).** **The survey done 2026-10-29 (REA on `ddi_2700g`, `libGLES_CL`, `d3d_2700g`: `2700g.md` "Dell's flip path, read"): Dell flips (never copies), triple-buffers by default, writes `GSCADR` then `DUCTRL` in the VEVENT0 interrupt from a 16-slot queue, never polls `DVLNUM` for flips, never moves VEVENT0. Weighed: at 149.50 / 2 the shipped order is already top down (checker only in divide-1 test images), and the frames that fall back are those after a longer frame (the spike rule's twice-the-last) or steadily over ~13.1 ms; a ping-pong between the screens breaks ADR-0034 rule 2; proposed instead: the fallback rendered into screen 1 and copied into screen 0 behind the scan (no flip; the copy planned by `Schedule::window` as a 3.1 ms top-down render: from line 557 for a whole screen with the scheduler's margins, not only in the 5-line blanking; projected from GC2DE: shown as soon as option 1, the core free ~13 ms sooner, no back-to-back signal needed), the copy's tear-freeness and the textured +15% by screen-1 address to be measured in the proof run; flips mid-scan found (R113). Waiting for the owner's verdict before the build.** **Ruled by the owner 2026-10-29: "Yes, copy variant" (ADR-0034): the fallback renders into screen 1 and is copied into screen 0 behind the scan, no flip; the back-to-back signal question dropped. From here every new fix or test gets a one-page alternatives sheet (original, Dell's from REA with addresses or a logged no-result search, modern, why ours wins, what overturns each) and the owner's go before it is built or run.** **The proof run built 2026-10-29 on the owner's go for its sheet (GC2DF, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_HALF_R20P`, `2700g.md` "R20's proof run built"): C93's copy start lines (with a negative control at line 400) and landing order, then the copy fallback, the blanking start (rows, checkerboard) and option 1, one-off and back to back, every refresh judged by `DSIG`; emulator-checked on rp-next-032; the device run waits for the owner's go.** **GC2DF ran 2026-10-29 (gpubench-0220): the copy 0 torn refreshes (48 one-off, 48 back to back, 32 C93 copies inside its window), shown 24.3 ms, core free 24.3 ms, 47.9 frames a second back to back; the blanking start and option 1 each tore one refresh in every frame (48 of 48); C93 holds (top down; begun too early, 23 of 24 torn: the negative control). | R22 | The CF card as a USB drive is slow in Windows Explorer: the drive's icon, its capacity bar and a folder's contents all take a long time to appear. Measure what Windows reads on attach (sectors, their order, each command's time) against the card path's 4.1-5.2 MB/s, then fix the slow part (polled reads, per-command latency, a read cache for the FAT and root directory). **Past work (2026-10-15/16), none of it this symptom:** item 83 (the card attached late: "no medium" at enumeration, then stalls; fixed, its with-Explorer-open device check never recorded), the blank drive (buffer front, proven: size at first connect), item 84 (a view from before the loader: the drive goes away 2-10 s on purpose), A5 (0.6 MB/s end to end, the USB side). **Leads to measure:** Windows' free-space scan reading the whole FAT for the capacity bar (an 8 GB FAT32 card's FAT is 1-8 MB by cluster size: 2-13 s at 0.6 MB/s, more if read in small commands); item 84's away window firing on a fresh mount; the first-enumeration fallback (udc.md). **The owner, 2026-10-24:** opening the drive, Explorer's green loading bar creeps across the address bar (folder loading: Explorer reading the directory and, for its folder-type guess and details, each file's start; plus the free-space scan). PC-side split, no image change: time `dir J:\` and `fsutil volume diskfree J:` against Explorer opening the same folder. **Measured 2026-10-24 (the drive already mounted, the CF card on K:):** `dir K:\` 0.04 s, `fsutil volume diskfree K:` 0.04 s, the whole tree (7,546 entries) 3.0 s: once Windows has read the card everything is fast, so the slowness is the first read after the drive appears (Windows' cache cold). The measurement to take: the loader's log of each command and its time from the moment the drive attaches. **The owner, same day: not only the first time; Windows frequently takes forever.** Likely cause: after every image run each card that changed goes away and comes back on purpose (item 84, so Windows can't write back a stale FAT: the noise-ri.bin loss), and every run changes the CF card (its run log, `rplog/`, written on the SD and CF cards), so every run makes Windows drop its cache and re-read the CF drive cold. To check in the loader's log: a medium change after each run. Better ways to measure: the run log on one card only, or a medium change only when the FAT or a directory Windows has cached actually changed **Built 2026-10-24 (the owner's ruling: make the log files once, and only make the drive vanish when the file list really changed; ADR-0014 rule 3 amended):** 32 slot files and `rplog/index.txt` made once per card; a run writes only inside its slot; the loader vouches a card the run never took or changed (logged "card: unchanged by the run"). Emulator: emu-cardlog passes (the CF card vouched after the SD test, the SD card it took not). Also found on the way: one text slice in rp-fat pulled ~4 KiB of Unicode tables into the loader; removed (loader 129,798 -> 123,606 B, no budget raise). To prove on the X51v: install both loaders, run a test image, and Explorer opens the CF drive at once after it. Cost: C78 (Windows may show an old copy of the slot file) | the owner, 2026-10-24 | image:rp-loader; device-run | open |

A write test in its own folder on the CF card (write at each faster timing, read back at the slowest), then the loader's write timing from it. **Run 2026-10-24 (`rp-storfast` C7, the owner's 8 GB card): the data right at every strobe to 0 (10 cycles), every sector exact; but writes with the data timing switched per sector ran 240 KiB/s against 4,637 at Windows Mobile's timing throughout: switching `MCMEM1` twice a sector costs ~1 ms (to measure). Switched once a command (2026-10-24, second run): **still 238-240 KiB/s at every faster data timing, every sector exact: not the switching.** Each sector costs ~2 ms more at any data timing with the tuned set-up and hold (1 and 1) instead of Windows Mobile's (8 and 8), even at Windows Mobile's strobe: next, the strobe shortened with Windows Mobile's set-up and hold, and the set-up and hold alone, to find which part the card slows for** **C9 on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/rp-storfast-C9.txt`):** one field at a time shorter than Windows Mobile's (strobe, hold, set-up, the rest kept): every step 231-236 KiB/s against 4,645, every sector right; once-a-command switching no faster than once a sector. The speed drops whenever the data phase's `MCMEM1` differs from the command's, whatever the value: next, each phase of a write timed, and `MCMEM1` rewritten to its own value (storage-speed.md) **Built 2026-10-25: C10** (`CfCard::write_blocks_marked` reports each phase): the same 64-sector write with the command, both switches, the DRQ waits, the data words and the finish timed, at Windows Mobile's data timing and at strobes 9 and 1, twice each; emulator-checked, waiting for the owner's go. (A rewrite of `MCMEM1` to its own value is already in the fast case: C7/C9's Windows Mobile rounds write the register around the data phase too.) **C10 on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/rp-storfast-C10.txt`): the slowness is the card rewriting the same 64 sectors (one ~132 ms wait for the first DRQ, at Windows Mobile's timing too), not the timing:** C7/C9 wrote Windows Mobile's round first, to fresh sectors. The timing's real effect: the data words 5,041 us at Windows Mobile's, 2,961 at strobe 1 (41% less). Next: C11, each timing writing fresh sectors, rounds alternated, and the rewrite cost on its own **Built 2026-10-25: C11** (a 512 KiB file reserved: twelve regions written once each with the timings rotated, one region rewritten six times, a fresh write after a read at the slowest timing and one at strobe 1 after the rewrites, all read back): emulator-checked (its CF card now 32 MiB, as the loader's 32 run-log slots take 8 MiB of each card), waiting for the owner's go. **C11 on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/rp-storfast-C11.txt`): fresh-sector writes 5,745-5,794 KiB/s at Windows Mobile's timing, 6,054 at strobe 9, 9,435 at strobe 1 (1.64x), every sector right; rewriting the same sectors again at once: ~227 KiB/s (a ~135 ms wait each) from the second rewrite on; a slowest-timing read changes nothing.** For the owner: the loader's write timing (options in storage-speed.md); and the rewrite cost as its own lead (the FAT, directories, the run log rewriting a sector a line) **Built 2026-10-25: C12** (a 1 MiB file: how long the card stays slow after six rewrites, ten fresh writes timed at Windows Mobile's timing and at strobe 1; a pause of 0-1,000 ms before a second rewrite; the CFA feature set from IDENTIFY and its erase commands, `CfCard::erase_sectors` and `write_without_erase_marked`, host-tested on a card with them and one refusing them): emulator-checked, waiting for the owner's go. **C12 on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/rp-storfast-C12.txt`): no lasting slow state (fresh writes right after rewrites at full speed, 9.4-9.6 MB/s at strobe 1); pauses up to 1 s don't help a rewrite; the card claims no CFA feature set and its ERASE SECTORS doesn't make the next write fast.** The rewrite cost is the card's: the gain is software writing the same sectors rarely. Waiting on the owner: the loader's write timing (A, B or C); next to measure: the rewrite cost by write size **Built 2026-10-25 (the owner's ruling A): the write timing by rule M, measured once per card at the attach on fresh sectors of `rplog/cfwrite.bin`, kept in `rplog/cfwrite.txt` (storage-speed.md); loader limit 124 -> 128 KiB (approved); and C13, the rewrite cost by size. Emulator-checked; waiting for the owner's go.** **C13 on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/rp-storfast-C13.txt`): a rewrite costs ~135-138 ms whatever its size (1, 8 or 64 sectors); a log's pattern (16 lines, two to a sector) 1.1 s, 8 lines over 20 ms.** The run log rewrites a sector every second line and the FAT a sector each file change: to plan, write each sector once (the run log keeping its partial sector in RAM, the FAT's updates batched) | Claude, 2026-10-24 (R7's follow-up) | image:rp-storfast; device-run | open |

| R42 | `cargo xtask emu-usb-suite` fails at `boot_loader_drive` with "drive: no answer": after the loader's `put` of retropane.bin, the PC side's command waiting for the medium to go away and come back gets no reply. Fails the same on main (2026-10-24, emulator release rp-v0.30.0) and on the R22 branch, twice each: not R22's doing. Find when it started (the last passing run), then fix the class (the loader stops serving the drive after a put?) with a guard. **2026-10-25:** a cause found and fixed: `Msc::medium_changed` cut off a transfer under way (it dropped the read-ahead and took the unit away mid-command), now held until the command ends (rp-usb-msc test `a_medium_change_mid_transfer_waits_for_the_command_to_end`); `emu-drive` now passes the put's away-and-back and ruling A's two blinks, and hangs later, in its hostile-input step (junk transfers, then a read past the end): the next lead **Resolved 2026-10-25:** two causes. (1) the mid-transfer medium change (fixed in rp-usb-msc); (2) the check's own 20 s per-command hang detector: the 1 MB read across the emulator's slow block takes 24.3 s on this PC, so that command gets 120 s (`Bot::wait`). `emu-drive` passes whole. | found 2026-10-24 by the R22 checks | session | done gate-overnight (R42 fix) |
| R43 | `emu-usb-suite`'s CF check (`boot_loader_cf`) failed once in three runs (2026-10-24): after the test pulled the card and pushed it back, the loader never attached it again (events: ready 0, dead, power off, power off; no second `attached`). The same check passed alone and in the next suite run; the attach itself failed, before the CF guard (ruling A) runs. Find why a re-insert can fail to attach (the detect debounce against the power-off timing?), fix the class, guard it **Found 2026-10-25:** the card dropped as dead when pulled; pushed back within the detect debounce (two readings a second apart), so no removal was seen ("detect changes 0"); the one automatic retry ran while the card was still out and failed ("was timeout"), and none was left. **Fixed (the class):** up to three retries per insertion, after 1, 3 and 10 s (`CF_RETRY_SECS`), still never a loop; `emu-cf` now asks `cf` when a re-insert fails. 3 of 3 runs passed after (2 of 3 failed before). To prove on the X51v: a quick pull and push **On the X51v 2026-10-25 (the owner's quick pull):** the card came back on its own (the retry fix works) but after ~17 s: pulled and pushed back in 0.12 s (READY 0 at 105.26 s, 1 at 105.38 s), the once-a-second detect debounce never saw it, a failing read found it at 117.3 s, attached again at 122.7 s. Not a hardware limit: the detect bit follows the card at once; our debounce is slow. Options to the owner: A, the detect bit every ~50 ms (two agreeing readings); B, READY falling while idle; C, the CPLD's latched card-changed flag (R24) **Option A built 2026-10-25 (the owner's choice):** the detect read every 50 ms (`CF_DETECT_TICKS`), two agreeing readings; `emu-cf` swaps the card in 0.15 s with nothing reading it and requires the loader to attach it again from the detect alone (fails with the old 1 s, a mutant guards it); the loader's reply line 256 -> 512 B (`cf` lost its detect count) and the slot's event list 12 -> 24. To prove on the X51v: the quick pull again **Option A on the X51v 2026-10-25:** the quick swap seen and the card attached again 1.5 s after the pull (loader events 49.14 -> 50.69 s); the PC's watcher (its own clock) saw Windows drop the drive at 11:12:38.3 and show it again at 11:12:58.0, 19.7 s later; the owner ~30 s to the icon. The rest is Windows: noticing, the loader's 2-10 s away (item 84), and the cold re-read. Options to the owner: A, a few seconds of "becoming ready" when a card is pulled and no medium change if the same card (identity, FAT and directory fingerprint) is back in time; B, R22's read cache; C, a shorter away (kept: it guards the noise-ri.bin loss) **Traced on the X51v 2026-10-25 (`local/cftrace-reseat-2026-10-25.txt`):** pull 50.2 s, attached 51.7 s, "medium changed" answered 53.3 s, then Windows read 31,440 sectors of the 15,838-sector first FAT (twice, 7.7 MB each, nothing else of size) at the USB link's ~1 MB/s, 54-70 s; the PC watcher's 19.6 s matches the loader's 19.8 s (the clocks agree). FSInfo valid (3,350 MiB free, as Windows says). Windows' two FAT passes and full-speed USB are not ours; the FAT's size is the card's format (4 KiB clusters): 32 KiB clusters would make it ~1 MB (~2 s). Ours: ~3 s (attach 1.5 s, handshake 1.6 s) | found 2026-10-24 by the CF read-write checks | session | open |

Find whether the PC or the loader drops a frame's last packet (a short last packet, a zero-length packet, the CDC side's flush), fix the class, guard it in the emulator | found 2026-10-25 installing the CF card's loader | session | open |
| R45 | Formatting cards in RetroPane (the owner, 2026-10-24: "i want retropane to be able to format cards too, both sd and cf"; parked late, recorded 2026-10-25): FAT32 with a chosen cluster size and exFAT, from the loader now and the OS later, only on the owner's explicit command, never a card's boot files without asking. Risk: Windows' cache then counts the boot file's clusters free, so its next file can be put there and refused. Options for the owner's vote (ADR-0014 rule 3): **A** medium changed at once after any refused write (Windows drops its cache; the file reappears in seconds), **B** leave it, **C** also set the boot files' read-only attribute (with A). Claude recommended A + C. **The owner's ruling (2026-10-25): "A"** (C not chosen), built and proven on the X51v the same day (the delete refused, the file back, byte-identical), **then withdrawn by the owner:** "i dont want the blink, i dont want it to look broken, bad user experience" (Explorer force-closed, and Windows asked to "scan and fix": the FAT's dirty flag left set). The blink is removed; the drive stays put after a refusal (the emulator checks assert it). **Open, for the owner's vote** (designs laid out 2026-10-25, modelled on how modern devices do it): (2) the boot files in a small boot partition offered to the PC write-protected (Windows shows its own "the disk is write-protected" dialog at once; the data partition fully writable, no guard), with the read-only attribute on top; (1) MTP for the OS proper (the PC asks the device, a refusal is an immediate error dialog, Android's way); (3) hidden + system attributes (a curtain, not a lock). Claude recommended 2 now, 1 for the OS proper; **revised after research the same day:** 2 is out (Windows Mobile shows only a card's first partition, XDA reports); recommended: (3, better) the boot files shown to the PC only as hidden + system + read-only by the loader rewriting those attribute bytes in the directory sectors it serves (the card untouched, Windows Mobile unaffected), the guard still the real lock; and for the OS proper (1, better) MTP for Windows with mass storage kept for Macs, Linux and old Windows (no support dropped), chosen automatically if Windows' MS OS descriptor request proves reliable, else a setting. **The owner (2026-10-25): "do your proposed fix".** Layer 1 built: the PC's view (ADR-0014 rule 3 amended; host tests on every format, emu-format). Layer 2: ADR-0037 (proposed). To prove on the X51v: Explorer hides the boot files and warns, nothing looks broken. **On the X51v 2026-10-25 (the owner):** the four boot files hidden in Explorer by default (42 items, everything else shown, the SD icon kept); with hidden and protected files shown, deleting default.txt gave Windows' own warning ("The file 'default.txt' is a system file... Are you sure you want to permanently delete it?"); on Yes, an immediate error popup: "Error 0x8007045D: The request could not be performed because of an I/O device error" (the refusal is visible at once, no fake delete). **Next:** the wording reads like a failing card: try the refusal's SCSI answers (DATA PROTECT with ASC 0x27 qualifiers, ILLEGAL REQUEST) one per step and keep the one Windows words as "write protected" or "access denied"; both dialogs' "Size: 0 bytes" is the dialog only (Windows reports 116 bytes after). Checked after (read-only): the card's default.txt byte-identical to the backup (SHA-256 898903f2...); Windows lists it Hidden, System, Archive **without Read-only**: before deleting a read-only file Windows clears the flag, that attribute write is the one refused (hence the I/O error) and Windows' cache kept its version; the wording experiment starts with that refusal. **Found the same day (Windows' System log):** after the refused delete, disk event 51 ("an error was detected during a paging operation") every ~22 s, 75 today: Windows' file system changed the entry in its cache before writing, the write was refused, and it keeps retrying the cached sector until the drive is re-read. A plain drive can't refuse a file honestly without this (the reason Android moved to MTP). Options put to the owner: A, MTP sooner (ADR-0037: the device answers before anything changes); B, a quiet re-sync (re-offer once after the PC goes idle; still closes an open Explorer); C, leave it (hidden + Windows' warning make it rare, a replug clears it). Claude recommended C now, A before the internal flash goes writable. **The owner's ruling (2026-10-25): "build c", then the same day "no wait build A, MTP sooner (ADR-0037)"**: MTP is built now, the real fix (the device answers a delete before anything changes, so a protected file gets an immediate "can't delete" with nothing left behind), mass storage kept for Macs and Linux; the hidden view stays meanwhile. Steps on the roadmap (ADR-0037) | the owner, 2026-10-25 | session; device-run | open |

Next: GC2CK, each TEX2 bit around the DOT3 words under ramps, flagging any output channel that follows another channel's texel (a real sum); fallback designs need many passes (the 32-bit frame read as 8-bit grey to move channels, weights by tint, sums by additive blending).** **GC2CK and GC2CL ran 2026-10-28: no single TEX2 bit sums channels, but REA showed Dell's DOT3 also sets TEX0 bit 13, and with it the core computes clamp(4 x texel . tint), replicated to all four channels, no bias, no negative weights. Next (rule 0): pick our own transform for the video plane's programmable converter (CSC01-05) with non-negative weights (U' = B and V' = R already in their UYVY bytes, Y' a DOT3 luma), so no bias and no sign are needed and the converter's inverse is computed from the quantized weights; measure DOT3's rounding, the placement of Y0/Y1 into the G and A bytes (2D engine ROP with a mask, or blending), and the passes' total cost; a lossless one-pixel-a-word layout (vertical scaling only) as the other choice.** **GC2CM ran 2026-10-28: the whole conversion works on the X51v (U' and V' exact in all 38,400 words; Y' follows DOT3's rounding floor(4 sum(t c) 255 / 65536), a fixed rule for the converter's inverse); 16.45 ms a frame at WM's clock (3D 3 x 4.08, 2D 2 x 2.11). Next: the cost down (divide 1, the 2D placement overlapped with the next render, fewer or 16-bit passes), the converter's registers read (CSC01-05) and the picture shown through the video plane's smooth 2x for the owner's eye, then R79's fitted filter: our own upscaler.** **Cost cut 2026-10-28 (GC2CN, GC2CO on the X51v): memory-bound (the cores' clock doesn't move it; one render costs what three do); 2D placements overlapped with the next frame's 3D: 13.05 ms a frame, exact. Next: the conversion on the system-clock ladder (99.67, 133.25, 143.00 MHz: the memory's clock is the lever), then the converter's registers (CSC01-05) and the picture through the video plane for the owner's eye.** **GC2CP built 2026-10-28 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_HALF_ROUTES`, 2700g.md "Run GC2CP built"), the owner's question "is the smooth 2x worth it?": GC2CA's four game frames at 74.75 / 1 and 143.00 / 1, native 480 x 640 against 480 x 320 (the video plane's lossless vertical-only RGB 2x) and 240 x 320 converted (serial with a full or a registers-only renderer switch, and pipelined: frame N's conversion while the CPU writes frame N + 1's lists), every picture checked, GC2CM's card converted at each clock; emulator-checked; waits for the owner's go.** **GC2CP ran 2026-10-28 ~4:25 PM (gpubench-0175, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2CP.txt`): the conversion exact at both divide-1 clocks; the registers-only renderer switch right and ~10 ms a frame cheaper than the full set-up; fastest whole frame against native: 480 x 320 (vertical-only RGB 2x, lossless) 1.56-2.06x, 240 x 320 converted 0.89-1.37x serial and 1.13-1.62x pipelined (the pipelined overlap leaves 2-72 stray words a frame); the times don't follow the clocks (native 18.7 ms and the conversion's render 12.25 ms at 74.75 / 1 and 143.00 / 1 alike): the renderer waits to its predicted end (R80 (2)), so GC2CN's memory-bound reading is withdrawn. The switch ships meanwhile; this only adds speed **The owner, 2026-10-30 night: "take 3 more test", then "do all tests that you want": tests 1-3 ran (gpubench-0237 to -0239; sheet `docs/research/sheets/r121-divide1-display-on.md`): the shipped layout (lists on die, the underlay) misses one pixel in 2 of 100 flat frames; an alpha-0 quad blended over the frame cures it at 143.00 / 1 (0 of 480) and is faster than R120's divide-2 switch on every frame (8.7 vs 9.2-11.3 ms), with no clock change. Both soaked an hour clean (R117T gpubench-0240: 16,572 round trips, 0 wrong; R121S gpubench-0241: 32,592 overlay renders, 0 wrong); which ships is the owner's** **The owner picked the overlay, 2026-10-30 morning ("Invisible layer"): `Renderer::set_overlay` (listed last in every tile, its blend in the prediction; switching it, like the underlay now, redraws nothing) and the service's `sdram_cures`, both off by default; owed: the device check through the service at 143.00 / 1 with fallback frames forced (the owner's go)** **scene-0008 (the owner's "Run it", 2026-10-30): through the service at 143.00 / 1 with the display on, the copy path with the cures 0 torn of 708 refreshes, without them (the control) 745 of 745; the targets switching inside a phase not yet exercised (rp-scene sends 2D+3D changes by the copy): 3D-alone frames added to P6 and the references compared below the label, emulator-checked; the second run waits for his go** **scene-0009: the cures change no pixel (bands below the label same as 149.50 / 2's), copy 0 of 709 with them, 745 of 745 without, 40 frames behind the scan with them switched off, 0 torn: done** | Claude, 2026-10-30 | after:owner | done scene-0009 |

Image `target/variants/c2b/rp-clocks.bin`; the device run waits for the owner, watching** **Run 1 on the X51v (2026-10-30, the owner watching): hung, the hardware watchdog reset it (Dell's restore screen); WM had left 624 (CCCR 0x02000310, code at the top). The card log (clocks-0002) kept only its first sector (C1's registers): the lines after were held back while the card programmed, so the step that hung is unknown. Class fix: `cardsink::flush()` (everything logged onto the card and programmed) before each risky step, in rp-clocks' C2 and C2b; run 2 waits for the owner's go** **Run 2 (2026-10-30, the owner watching): the green stripe (step 10, the 624 control: no clock change yet) stayed, then the watchdog reset it; the card log (clocks-0003) again only its first 512 bytes, though the run reached C2b: the card log itself stops after its first sector in this image (the flush didn't help), and the hang came at or right after the first flush/thermometer read, before any overclock. The overclock is untested; the cause is being reproduced in the emulator (emu-cardlog with the C2b variant) before any run 3** **Run 3 (2026-10-30): a solid green screen, then the watchdog (the owner's photo). Green is the loader's own start-up beacon (step 2, "MMU on", before the 2700G answers), as the owner reported from run 2 on and Claude misread as the test's stripe: the test was taken back to the loader mid-run and the loader then hung at start-up. Likely chain, not proven: the SD card stays busy after the card log's first sector in this image, every later card-log write waits on it (`cardsink::flush` up to five settles of seconds each, with no check-in) past the loader's 30 s soft run limit, the loader restarts and hangs on the same card, the hardware watchdog resets. STOPPED: no run 4 until (1) card waits are bounded under the run limit (a check-in while waiting, never seconds blocked), (2) why the SD card stays busy in rp-clocks is found, (3) the loader never hangs at start-up after a run-limit return. The overclock itself is untested** **Fix (1) done: `cardlog::SETTLE_TICKS`, a card wait gives up after one second (host test); (2) and (3) open: why the SD card stays busy in rp-clocks, and the loader's `open_card` after a stopped run (the likely green-screen hang)** **PARKED by the owner, 2026-10-30 ("park this for now and lets start the design work"): resume with the two open items before any run 4** **Resumed 2026-11-16 (the owner: "Yes, start C2") in the port runner (`-rpcpu`, a36ecf27). Put back to 624 by a soft reset. | R126 | The Classic desktop's icon art (ui.md "The Classic desktop", vote D5): the owner picks who makes the XP/Vista-style set (his own, an artist's with permission, or an open set); check the open candidates' licences and look (Everaldo's Crystal Clear and Nuvola, LGPL as remembered) and that they cover the shell's programs; never Microsoft's icons. **Checked 2026-10-30 (Commons pages and web, not the original archives):** Crystal Clear (Everaldo Coelho) LGPL per Commons and its kde-look posting, the author never confirmed it directly to others who asked (Drupal thread), some icons carry other companies' logos (AIM, Yahoo, Tux, KDE: never shipped); Nuvola (David Vignoni) LGPL 2.1 in its own README, 2003-04; Oxygen (KDE) LGPL 3 per KDE (packagers list LGPL 2), some icons carry the KDE logo (trademark); Tango public domain but flat (owner rejected its look for weather). No maintained glossy XP/Vista-style open set exists: all are 2003-08. Third-party art ships as a pack, never in the base (ui.md); the base needs its own set either way. Owner's pick (2026-10-30): the mock-up icons' look, not Claude's drawings; open: who makes the set (the owner or an artist); the list to cover is in ui.md. Checked 2026-10-30 (owner's find): Directory Opus Vista Icons v2.0 by Cris van Minnen (resource.dopus.com/t/3986): no licence stated (so all rights reserved), toolbar/action icons for a file manager rather than program icons, built into Directory Opus 10+ since (rights may now sit with GPSoftware too), and whether any are drawn from Microsoft's Vista icons is unstated; usable only with the author's written permission naming RetroPane's scope and confirming they are his own drawings. **The owner's target look (2026-10-30): Windows Vista's own icon style** (glass, perspective, real objects, aqua tints; he sent Vista's icons, knowing Microsoft's can't be used). Open sets searched for it: **Oxygen (KDE, LGPL 3, 2008-now, maintained in KDE Frameworks, realistic objects and glass, thousands of icons incl. calendar, mail, notes, PDA, folders, file types) is the closest**; Echo (Fedora, CC BY-SA 3.0, 2007, abandoned, partial); Crystal Clear/Project and Nuvola (LGPL, rounder and cartoonier); Vista Inspirate (Saki, 2005) and SnowIsh: no licence found; Iconshock Vista sets not open. Oxygen's app icons for other projects (digiKam, Akonadi) and any KDE logo are never used: its generic names (office-calendar, internet-mail, pda...) only. In the base it needs the owner's exception to the packs rule (as for VClouds); LGPL 3 sits with GPL-2.0-or-later as GPL 3. **Style brief from the owner's second sheet (Vista's own shell icons, 2026-10-30, Microsoft's, reference only):** folders as teal-green glass seen at three-quarters, open at the front like a book, with what's inside shown standing in them (a note, music, film, a star, pictures); badges over a base icon (a check, a magnifier, an arrow, a lock) rather than new drawings; the device itself drawn as glass with a bright screen; a glass recycle bin; light from the top left, soft reflections, one family of tints (teal, aqua, a little green). Oxygen differs: blue, flatter folders, no glass. Option to weigh: Oxygen restyled (its folders re-tinted to teal glass, Vista-like badges composited), a derivative it allows under LGPL 3 and credited as such. **Owner chose an artist (2026-10-30).** Brief to send: docs/design/icon-brief.md. Where to look (searched 2026-10-30): the 2006-era DeviantArt Vista-style artists (Chad Jackson, vinis13, aroche) are inactive, their sets carry no licence and some redraw Microsoft's icons: dead ends; live leads are today's Frutiger Aero community (Dribbble's frutiger-aero tag, Figma Community, frutigeraeroarchive.org, sellers on Gumroad, itch.io and Iconscout), freelance icon designers (Dribbble hire), and studios that take custom work (Aha-Soft says it does; the Iconfactory drew Vista's own icons, commissions unconfirmed). The owner sends every message. **Linux distributions' sets checked (owner asked, 2026-10-30):** today's distro sets are flat (Breeze, Adwaita, Papirus, Yaru); the glossy era's: Oxygen (KDE 4, Kubuntu, openSUSE 11; LGPL 3; closest), Crystal SVG/Clear (KDE 3, SUSE 10, Linspire; LGPL; Linspire branding left in some), Echo (Fedora 8-10; CC BY-SA 3.0; unfinished), Ubuntu's Human/Glossy (2008; licence to check), Faenza and elementary (GPL 3; soft, not glass), Pardus Glass-3 (licence field empty, the author invites use: ask), Mandriva Elegant (licence not found); Windows-look themes like B00merang's Windows-7 icons likely carry Microsoft's own icons: never. ReactOS (open-source Windows clone) once shipped an icon identical to Vista's and replaced it (2007): its art needs per-file checks. No distro shipped a Vista-glass set; the artist route stands. **KDE Modern Classic (owner's find, store.kde.org/p/2379533, checked 2026-10-30):** LGPL 3; 1,196 SVG icons (3,062 names) redrawing KDE 2/3's "KDE Classic" set (its artwork free for any use) plus new ones (weather, tray, settings); glossy colours, top-left light, dark outlines, teal arrows: the KDE 3 look of 2000-04, not Vista glass; work in progress; the store page says every icon was drawn as SVG by Claude under its author's direction and review (the owner has ruled out Claude-drawn icons for RetroPane: his call whether a third party's AI-drawn set counts); its K-menu icons carry the KDE logo (trademark): never used. **Crystal Diamond (the owner likes it, 2026-10-30; store.kde.org/p/1002592, v2.85, 2012, Paolo Campitelli):** a compilation, not original art: its page says "The icons are not mine!" and names Crystal Project and Crystal Clear (Everaldo, LGPL), Vista Inspirate and Nuove XT (Saki, no licence found), Realistik, an OSX set by kiddo (likely Apple's art), Debian icons and "many other", plus distribution and company logos (Apple, Ubuntu and the rest: trademarks). ALT Linux packages it as "GPL", but a compiler can't relicense others' icons. Now `cargo xtask load` puts the PC's time on the card at every load (`RPTIM`: UTC minutes, the offset, a CRC; `rp_today::clock::card_time`), the image starts its clock from it (`clock::from_card`, checked as hostile input; the build's time only without it) and logs which. Not proven which: the X50v's USB transceiver route (`UP2OCR`/port selection), its cable-detect GPIO, or the cradle's link. **The card log read 2026-11-13 (on the X51v): this boot left none.** The loader writes the card log only for an image's run; its own start-up events stay in RAM and on its screen, so the photo is the whole record (the CF card's newest log is gpubench-0137 of 2026-11-12). **Dell's X50v USB driver read 2026-11-13 (9ef53638, udc.md "The X50v"):** it switches CPLD `0x1C` bit 3 itself (on with the cable) and the USB client clock, which the X51v's doesn't; the loader keeps `0x1C` as Windows Mobile left it, so the lead (unproven) is bit 3 off under Windows Mobile 2003. **The loader's start-up log built 2026-11-13 (sheet `docs/research/sheets/r193-loader-startup-log.md`, `hal/x51v/src/bootlog.rs`, udc.md "The X50v"):** at its first start after HaRET the loader keeps `rplog/` `loader-NNNN` on each card: `CKEN`, `PSSR`, the GPIO words, `UDCCR`/`UP2OCR`/`UP3OCR`, the CPLD shadow raw with its verdict and `0x1C` (bus, WM slot, kept, record; bit 3 named), then each USB event for 5 minutes or until the PC configures; reads only, no board bit changed. Emulator-proven (`emu-cardlog`: on both cards alike, closed at configuration). **Run on the X50v 2026-11-13 (the owner's go; `local/cardlog/loader-0001.txt`, CF card):** the log works on the device (5 minutes, 89 sector writes, 0 refused, closed at 5 minutes). The UDC side matches the working X51v exactly: `UP2OCR` `0x00020000` (on-chip transceiver, `HXOE`, no on-chip pull-up), `UDCCR` UDE on, so the transceiver set-up is ruled out. CPLD `0x1C` bit 3 is off everywhere (bus 0000, WM slot 0004, kept 0004), and Windows Mobile 2003's shadow table is at `0xAC001320` as on WM5 (taken as WM's). `CKEN` bit 11 was off as found. **Setups 0 in 5 minutes; the 37 resets come one per loader replug** (plus 2 at start): the loader's own unplug (SE0 through the host controller) explains them, so the PC most likely never saw the device at all. That points to the D+ pull-up: hard-wired on the X51v, likely switched by CPLD `0x1C` bit 3 on the X50v (Dell's X50v driver sets it with the cable; AxDroid's X50 board file sets it at boot). GPIO14 read 1 at the start and 0 after 1.7 s: the X51v's cable pin isn't the X50v's. **Bit 3 run built 2026-11-13 (the owner's "go, build way A"; sheet `docs/research/sheets/r193-x50v-cpld1c-bit3.md`):** opt-in by HaRET's command line (`rp.x50v-usb-bit3`, read from the ATAG list `r2` points at, kept through the relocation and `_start`; `hal/x51v/src/atags.rs`), from the CF card's own `RetroPaneX50v` folder that `cargo xtask install cf` now writes; the X51v booting `RetroPane` sees an empty command line and changes nothing. The loader sets `0x1C` bit 3 at 30 s (shadow, WM slot and board record), replugs, and the start-up log now also has a line per GPIO level change (noisy pins cut after 8) and the GPLR words every 10 s. Emulator-proven (`emu-cardlog`: without the opt-in no bit, with `=0` the bit before USB and the PC configures). **PROVEN on the X50v 2026-11-13 (`loader-0002`, report `docs/reports/runs/2026-11-13-loader-0002-x50v-bit3.md`): bit 3 is the X50v's USB switch:** off for 30 s, 0 setups; on at 30.8 s, the PC configured the device at 33.1 s and listed the loader's serial port; a cable pull and replug re-enumerated with no software step. **Next:** (1) the X50v's board detection (a fact that tells the X50v from the X51v, read from the two units' start-up logs) so the loader sets bit 3 on the X50v by itself, no opt-in; (2) the cable pin: keep the start-up log's GPIO lines running past configuration (or a GPIO-only log) and pull the cable again; (3) the drive layout on the X50v. Then close R193. **2026-11-13 later: the X50v on its own cards.** The kit (`RetroPaneX50v`, bit 3 at the start) was copied onto the X50v's own CF card through Windows Mobile Device Center (Windows Mobile 2003 in its cradle; read back byte-identical), and the loader started from it reached the PC with the serial + drive layout: configured 3 s after bit 3. `cargo xtask board` and `ls cf:` answer over the cradle, with no card swaps. In the cradle Windows Mobile 2003 had already set bit 3 (WM slot `000C`, ActiveSync connected); out of it, `0004`. **The loader tells the units apart (built 2026-11-13, sheet `docs/research/sheets/r193-x50v-detect.md`):** CPLD `0x06`, a status input with no latch, reads `0x0002` on the X51v (15 runs) and `0x000B` on the X50v (3 boots); `board::unit` takes only those exact values, and on an X50v the loader sets bit 3 at the start by itself (no folder or command line needed); unknown boards keep today's behaviour. Emulator: `unit: X51v`, nothing switched. **Proven on the X50v 2026-11-13 (loader-0003, report `docs/reports/runs/2026-11-13-loader-0003-x50v-detect.md`):** from the plain `RetroPane` folder the loader named the X50v and switched bit 3 on by itself; the PC configured the serial + drive layout at 1.4 s. On the way, a second fault was found and fixed (c66a0693): Windows Mobile 2003 with ActiveSync connected hands over UDCCR `0x808` (EMCE set), which every enable took as its own table's fault ("USB unavailable"); the UDC now clears a stale EMCE before its table. Still open: the X51v's start-up log saying `unit: X51v` (next X51v loader start from the new card copy), and the X50v's cable pin. Windows Mobile 2003 can't read the 4 GB SDHC card, so X50v runs start from the CF card | the owner, 2026-11-13 | session; device-run | open |

| R200 | **A D-pad button held across the tick counter's wrap doesn't repeat** (found 2026-11-14 reading the key code for `emu-apps`; not hit, not fixed: outside the core-apps order): rp-today calls `Keys::update(rt::ticks() / TICKS_MS, ...)`; the tick counter is 32 bits at 3.25 MHz, so the milliseconds it gives wrap at 1,321,528 (every 22 minutes), not at 2^32, while `rp_drv_pxa_keypad::keys` compares times with 32-bit wrapping arithmetic. A button pressed in the last half second before a wrap gets no repeat (and a program button no press-and-hold) until it is let go. The class: every caller that divides the tick counter and then does wrapping arithmetic on the result. Fix: feed the keys the loop's own 64-bit millisecond count (`now_ms`) truncated to 32 bits; audit the other `rt::ticks() / TICKS_MS` uses; a host test that steps the time across the wrap. Fixed 2026-11-15, the class: rp-today feeds the keys its loop's own milliseconds; `boot/hello` (keys and the pen's filter), `services/audio-s2` and `services/audio-x51v` (the jack's debounce) take `rt::uptime_ms`, which wraps at 2^32 only; the keys' own arithmetic was already tested across that wrap (`keys_are_independent_and_survive_the_clock_wrapping`) and its `update` now says what time it must be given. Left as it is: the picture maker's clock in rp-today (`rt::ticks() / 13 * 4`, a decode's time in a log line, wrong once in 22 minutes) | found 2026-11-14 writing `emu-apps` | session | done 2026-11-15 (every caller on a clock that wraps at 2^32) |

| R209 | **emu-today's look check: one whole-screen frame of 55,368 us against its 45,000 us** (2026-11-15, the emulator's times; the look switch back to dark on Weather's settings). The cause, measured: the 2D renderer wrote each row of glass it had not kept with four software divisions a column (`gradient_at`), about 140 us a row, and a dark glass screen writes 120 to 165 rows when it is drawn anew (the frame's queueing 18,353 us for 121 rows; 25,562 us for 165 on opening the screen). Fixed: the rows are stepped (`rp_gfx::render::Gradient`: each channel's quotient and remainder carried, four divisions a row, the same values column for column by test; one colour is not computed at all). After, the same frames: 39,960 us (queueing 2,947 us for the same 121 rows), the light switch 44,284 -> 38,180 us, the screen's opening 25,740 us -> under the slow-frame line; the look check passes. | R247 | **"I also need Google Calendar implementations"; "I want anyone who wants to sign into their Google account to be able to have it sync up their information, same for when we make an email app ... not a whole ordeal that people need to set up with OAuth individually"** (the owner, 2026-11-15). His requirement: sign-in is turnkey for every user; no user ever makes a developer client. What that takes (searched 2026-11-15, to be checked against Google's own pages before building): RetroPane OS itself is registered with Google once, by the project (one OAuth client shipped in the OS, not one a user); Google caps an unverified app at 100 users and wants its verification for Calendar and Contacts, and for full Gmail access a yearly security assessment (CASA; Google's pages give 500 to 4,500 dollars, other guides more); the X51v has no browser, so the user signs in on a phone or PC and the device gets the token (Google's device flow may not allow the Calendar scope: not confirmed), or a PC companion holds it; and the device needs its own Wi-Fi and TLS to sync away from the dock. The road with no registration at all: the standard protocols (CalDAV, CardDAV, IMAP) with an app password, which iCloud, Fastmail and others take; whether personal Google accounts still do is not confirmed. To decide with him before any code: who registers the project with Google and when; whether mail starts on the standards  **Corrected the same day (the owner: "every free email app that lets you sync your Gmail has to pay $500 a year, I think this is wrong"): he is right.** Google's own page on restricted scopes puts the yearly assessment on apps that reach that data "from or through a third-party server"; a mail program that talks to Gmail from the user's own device and keeps the mail there (as Thunderbird and the phone mail apps do) is not that. So the design rule for ours: mail, calendar and contacts go between the device (or the user's own PC) and Google only, never through a server of the project's; then registration and Google's review are the whole of it, and free. One community post says Gmail's review may still ask for the assessment: Google's review team is the one to ask when the mail app is designed. | owner, 2026-11-15 | after:the owner's ruling on the project's Google registration | open |

| R252 | The port layer's clock (`rp_time_us`, hal/cabi `gettimeofday`, 2026-11-15) counts from the program's start: C's `time()` gives seconds from 1970 as if the program had started then, so a game's save files and logs carry no real date. The real date needs the PXA270's real-time clock read (and set from the PC or the network) by the OS, then handed to `rp_port::sys::System` as the clock's starting second. Checked from C in the emulator only | Claude, 2026-11-15 | after:the OS keeps the date (the real-time clock driver) | open |
| R253 | A C game's sound (`rp_port::sound`, 2026-11-15) is woken only by the game's own port-layer calls: nothing wakes the mixer while the game is busy (a frame longer than the sound it queued is silence; the engine stops itself, never a stutter), and while a C program runs nothing drives the audio service's jack watch (ADR-0032 A2: a game's sound should pause when the headphones come out), its idle sleep, or the early wake. A C program that calls `exit()` instead of returning leaves the codec powered (the image's exit path has no way to the service). The proper waker is the audio host's own thread (ADR-0032 R1) or a timer in the game's image: which one follows from how a big program is run (half-life-readiness.md line 6). In `rp-port-hello` sound also needs a card (the system without one has no sound) | Claude, 2026-11-15 | after:the ADR on how a big program is run (half-life-readiness.md line 6) | open |
| R254 | A C game's input (`rp_port::input`, 2026-11-15) is read only when the game polls or reads its clock (at most every 4 ms): a button pressed and let go between two reads is missed (the keypad's activity latch says something happened, not which button), and the stylus is sampled once a frame, so the touch filter needs two or three frames to report a landing (it wants samples that agree). The shell reads both by interrupt. The proper reader is the same waker the sound needs (R253). Also: the stylus is mapped by the default calibration (`Calibration::X51V_DEFAULT`), not the user's own alignment, which the port layer has no way to yet; and the keypad's interrupt enable is left set when the C image returns to the loader | Claude, 2026-11-15 | after:the ADR on how a big program is run (half-life-readiness.md line 6) | open |
| R255 | The Half-Life evaluation build (`cargo xtask xash-eval`, docs/research/half-life-evaluation.md, 2026-11-15) links but for ten calls the port layer's C boundary (`hal/cabi`, `rp_port::sys`) does not answer: `stat`, `opendir`, `readdir`, `closedir`, `mkdir`, `rename`, `fsync`, `dup`, `getcwd`, `execve` (a refusal: a program cannot restart itself here). The engine's file system also opens files for reading and writing at once and for appending, both refused today (`rp_port::files` writes a file whole on close). The port layer also has no call to sleep (the engine's `Platform_Sleep` waits on the clock) and none to put a picture on the screen | Claude, 2026-11-15 | session | done (2026-11-15, the commit "The file calls an engine's own file layer asks": all ten answered, append and change-in-place opens too; `xash-eval` reports 0 unanswered; what is left is R259) |
| R256 | Not fetched, each a download that needs the owner's go (docs/research/half-life-evaluation.md): **FreeVGUI** (the Half-Life SDK's submodule, FWGS's: the client library's in-game panels, 264 names unanswered without it), **mainui** (the engine's menu; without it the engine starts in its console), and, only if he wants the Steam copy's music, **mpg123** (MP3). Fetched in this pass without asking again, as part of the Xash3D FWGS he approved: its `library_suffix` submodule (FWGS's own platform header, 14 files), which nothing builds without | Claude, 2026-11-15 | session | done (the owner, 2026-11-15: "yes, do whatever you need to get it going": FreeVGUI 73bfb465, mainui 8981066a with its miniutl 048a416f, and mpg123 20c59b07 fetched at the commits their repositories pin; building them in is R264) |

| R280 | **Misaligned reads: the X51v and the emulator may not agree, and the Half-Life port is C code from a lineage that reads words from odd addresses** (found preparing R278, 2026-11-15; a question, nothing seen). The runtime turns the MMU and caches on and leaves CP15's alignment-fault bit as it finds it (`hal/pxa27x/src/rt.rs`); on the PXA270 a word read from an address that is not a multiple of four faults with the bit set and reads a rotated word with it clear; whether the QEMU fork does either is not written in docs/hw/emulator.md. The build is ARMv5TE, where the compiler never makes such a read itself, so only a cast in the source can. In the emulator under the loader, all three against the compiler's: 136,320 results each, none differ; the emulator's cycles (not the X51v's) add 23.2 generic, 16.2 Rust, 13.8 assembly; multiply 21.2, 9.0, 6.2. `-rpfloat` times them on the X51v (run 6). Left of layer 2: divide (a reciprocal table and one Newton step), the conversions and compares, doubles, the relaxed game float (no subnormals or NaNs, where an exactness check against the float build allows it); layers 1 and 3 (the fixed-point renderer on the 2700G, the iWMMXt fixed-point library) where the samples point **Run 6 on the X51v (port-runner-0006, 2026-11-16): the frame 266 ms, 3.7 a second (run 5: 315 ms, 3.1).** Core clock cycles a call on the X51v: add 132.1 generic, 89.6 Rust, 55.7 assembly; subtract 132.9, 89.2, 56.6; multiply 91.8, 72.0, 48.9; 136,320 results each compared on the device, none differ. In the play's samples the assembly add 14.3% and multiply 13.9%, and a multiply's rare-case road (`mul_special` with `rp_fmul_rs`) 6.4%: a game multiplies by zero often, and the assembly hands every zero to Rust: next, zero in the assembly's own road; then divide (5.7%, still the compiler's) **Run 7 on the X51v (port-runner-0007, 2026-11-16, divide in assembly and a multiply's zero in the assembly's road, 844e1253): the frame 240 ms, 4.1 a second (run 6: 266 ms).** Divide on the X51v: 149.4 cycles generic, 190.8 Rust, 111.8 assembly, every result the same. Our assembly routines leave the return address alone, so the samples name their callers at last: the software renderer's `D_DrawSpans16` is 10.3% itself and calls 35% of the multiplies, 36% of the adds, 71% of the divides and 65% of the float-to-integer conversions, about 27% of the frame in all; with the blit to the screen (`R_BlitScreen` 7.0%) the CPU's drawing is about a third of the frame. | R325 | **Half-Life: an alien killed the player in c1a0c after the cascade, one the owner says should not be there** (the owner's play run on kit 76, 2026-11-17: "i got killed by an alien that wasnt supposed to be there after the cascade"). The cause is not known: the run log filled at the change to c1a0c (R326), so that part is lost. Candidates to rule out by evidence: the test setup (c1a0e started on its own, so c1a0c loaded with no saved state of its own from earlier in the game: "Couldn't open save data file save/c1a0c.HL1"; the suit given by `-rpsuit`), or the port (a trigger, global state or monster spawn that behaves differently). Found from the map itself (c1a0c.bsp's entity list, read only): two headcrabs belong there by design, one placed and one teleported in by a `monstermaker` (`teleport_spawner`) amid green beams; no other alien, no crowbar (the game gives it in c1a1), one health kit. The owner, 2026-11-17: it was a Vortigaunt ("the hive mind alien that shoots the beams"). c1a0c places none; c1a0e's cascade makes four in the test chamber (`A`-`D_teleport_spawner`, monster_alien_slave, from the `*_teleport_mm1` managers) and never removes them (its `kill_slave_stuff` kills only `slaves`, the four in the Xen vision), and c1a0e and c1a0c share the chamber through the landmark `c1a0etoc`, so a level change carries monsters standing near it. Open: whether GoldSrc carries them too (then the game is as made) or the port carries what it should not, or the cascade's timing at 4.6 fps fires spawners the original would not reach before `change_level` (10 s after the black room). Then: c1a0e to c1a0c in the emulator from a real save chain (c1a0 played through by a script, or the owner's own save), the monsters spawned in c1a0c listed against the map's own entity list. | Claude, 2026-11-17 | device-run | open |

| R328 | **Doom 3 on the X51v?** (the owner, 2026-11-18: "could we get doom 3 working on the x51v do you think?"). First answer, an estimate not a test: the real game's minimum was 384 MB of RAM and a 1.5 GHz CPU with an FPU; one level's data is hundreds of MB against the X51v's 64 MB, and the engine's per-frame float work (skinning, shadow volumes, physics) is far heavier than Half-Life's. The 2700G (MBX Lite) has DOT3 combining for bump-mapped light; its stencil support is unverified. The stall 3 min after overlap came on (frame 11,442): **bit 5 three times while a render ran** (status 0x00d5006c), then the TA never said done though bit 5 had been answered with 0x804 = 1 after the render: the TA does stop for parameter memory, and Dell's answer alone did not free it. The core's reset after it wedged the chip (the next chain's DMA stuck at descriptor 97, status reads garbage), and the program froze 30 s to a run-limit report (FAULT RunLimit in `irq_save`): every access waited out the 35 ms bus time-out and the 1 ms pump read the status each tick, so the rebuild was never reached. At each stall the chip did not answer (status reads garbage, 3.6-9.4 ms), the scenes held 46-127 pages (never short). **The owner's lead: "the texture glitching, hitching, and checkerboard issues especially happen when the flashlight is turned on"**: the flashlight is a dynamic light: ref_rp rewrites the lit faces' lightmaps in the 2700G's texture pages every frame (R297 B's `lm_dlit`), CPU writes into chip memory while, with the overlap, a render still reads those pages and a chain's DMA feeds the TA: the same bus, three users. The silence again began in the chain's first ~100 descriptors. No fps to compare (standing still, 54 fps; run 297: 45.7, run 275: 44.9). Next: the owner's 10-minute play, `-rpplay -rptexhold 300`, steps sent first and started only on his ready  **The lead after reading the chain and Dell's driver (session f71b5a8a, 2026-11-21): the core's event bit 9, counted since run 281 and never answered.** Run 295 (standing, no stall): 5 events; runs 298, 297, 293 (1, 6 and 4 stalls): 111, 1,329 and 1,768. A stall begins 40 to 80 descriptors into the TA's words (the chain's first 10 descriptors clear the tail and the regions, the next ~16 are the start table, then the words: stuck at 73, 67 and ~100 in runs 276, 278, 298), not spread along the chain as a page request anywhere would be. Dell's handler turns bit 9 into a flag (`ddi_2700g` `0x021192b4`, `+0xc4`) that its GLES acts on before feeding the TA again (`libGLES_CL` `0x0227038c`: the scene ended early with `0xF0000000` under `0x85C` bit 7, the TA's done waited for, `0x834` = 1, bits 6 and 7 waited for, then the render), and Dell asks the TA's FIFO for room before every word. The reading (unproven): a TA that raised bit 9 stops taking words until answered; our DMA fills its FIFO behind it, the next write holds the bus for good, and every access after it times out: the silent chip. Built (emulator-checked, not on the X51v): each event's place in its chain counted (none in flight, the first 128 descriptors, later), at a stall the time since the last event, and the lever `-rpev9` (`0x834` = 1 once no render is in flight). Next: one unattended walk without the lever (do the events sit at a scene's start; is one always just before a stall), then the same with `-rpev9`; the stop rule: events early in the chain and one within ~100 ms before every stall keeps the lead, a stall with none closes it  **Run 300 (2026-11-21, ab014038, the scripted walk; 5 min, the owner's soft reset):** the walk reached the door to c2a5e after 27 s and the level change failed (`GL_AllocTexture: MAX_TEXTURES limit exceeds`, R354), the game back at its menu, where it stayed (the owner: "it went into menu and then stopped working"). **In the menu event bit 9 came about once a frame: 1,932 in under a minute (122 in a chain's first 128 descriptors, 1,809 later), status `0x248` (bits 3, 6, 9) with a render running**, and one stall: the chain cut at descriptor 1,172, **the last bit 9 event 67.6 ms before the cut, 4 ms after that chain's arm**; rebuilt in 244 ms, the window's slowest frame 342 ms. The menu writes no texture, draws no model and has no flashlight, so none of those is the trigger; what it has is a render still running when the next frame's TA runs, every frame (72 fps of small frames). The reading now: bit 9 (with bit 6, the manager's) is a render ending while the TA runs, and a stall is that moment going wrong about once in several hundred; the ring's lost pages would be the same moment's lesser fault. `-rpmenu` (the game sent to its menu 3 s into the map) is the test bed with nobody playing. Next: run 301 `-rpmenu` 3 min without the lever, run 302 the same with `-rpev9`; the stop rule: 2 or more stalls in 301 against 0 in 302 names Dell's answer as the cure; stalls in both closes that lever and leaves the TA's start held until the render in flight has ended in just the frames where it has not (counted, its cost measured)  **Runs 301-303 (2026-11-21, the menu, nobody playing; the reading above overturned):** 301 crashed a frame after the disconnect (a data abort in the code hint reading the last scene's freed entities: fixed 8edc9432). **302, the bare menu, 3 min:** 392 strips a frame at 50 fps, a render still running at the next frame's start in 246 of 261 frames (18 ms), **4 bit 9 events, 0 stalls**. **303, the console's text over the menu, 3 min:** 934 strips a frame at 74 fps, no render ever waited for, **about 8,000 bit 9 events (428 in a chain's first 128 descriptors, 7,555 later; status `0x248` with a render running, `0x200` idle), 0 stalls**. So bit 9 follows what is drawn (many small see-through quads: text, and in play the effects, decals and HUD), not a render ending under the TA; and neither the events alone (303) nor the overlap alone (302) stalls. Run 300's one stall had both. What is left: the two together (a TA meeting whatever bit 9 says while a render is in flight), which play has in fights. Next: run 304, the walk with the flashlight and the gluon beam held (`-rpwalk -rpfx`: effects every frame over heavy scenes), 10 min, then the same with `-rpev9` if it stalls; if a scene with both never stalls in 10 minutes the lead closes  **Run 304 (2026-11-21, c3a9f1ec, `-rpwalk -rpfx`: the walk with the flashlight and the gluon beam held, 10 min, nobody playing):** 0 stalls, 2 bit 9 events, 5,250+ frames. So this scene, heavy and with a dynamic light and effects every frame, has neither the events nor the stall; the lead (the events and a render in flight together) is neither kept nor closed by it. Standing: stalls have come only in free play (runs 287-298) and in run 300's menu with the error's console over it; each now costs 0.34 s. Next, in order: (1) a free play by the owner with the new counts, since only play reproduces it (each stall's line says how long before it the last bit 9 came and where in its chain); (2) `-rpmenutext` again for 10 minutes with the overlap forced (a slow render under the text: the two suspects together, nobody playing)  **Run 305 (2026-11-21, the owner's 10-minute play, c3a9f1ec with c85fd327's decals): 43.4 fps over 25,750 frames (run 297: 45.7; run 275: 44.9); 14 stalls** (run 297: 6), each cut at descriptor 75 to 172 and rebuilt, the frames 205 to 431 ms (three in a row made one of 1.7 s); **4,440 bit 9 events** (2,039 in a chain's first 128 descriptors), the last one before a stall 97 to 170 ms earlier in 10 of 13, 0.3 to 3 s in the rest. His video (`IMG_0661.MOV`, the start spot, the flashlight on, standing still): the alcove's wall and floor drawn for a frame or two with other pictures (a lighter striped one, a piece of sky), back and forth; the same view's end shot, drawn with the chip waited for, is clean. **Run 306 (the console over the menu, 3ea51961's class order: solid primitives fed first, blended after): 12,481 events in 3 min (run 303: ~8,000), 1 stall: the order the TA is fed in does not change the events; that idea is closed as a cure** (the change stays behind `-rpnosort`, its picture not yet looked at on the X51v). What the menu runs share: an event a frame exactly when text is drawn; text, decals and the effects' sprites are the ARGB4444 pictures (their state block's TEX0 format 4 and TEX2 `0x86084610`, the texel's alpha), the bare menu's pictures are not. Next: one run of the same test with those drawn under the plain TEX2 (`-rptex2plain`, a probe: the text blocky), the stop rule: events near 0 names the 4444 state words as the trigger (then Dell's combine table for that format read against ours), events unchanged closes it  **Runs 307-309 (2026-11-21, the console over the menu, nobody playing):** 307, the ARGB4444 pictures under the plain TEX2 (`-rptex2plain`): 7,766 events, unchanged: that word is not the trigger (closed). **308 and 309, the driver's own search (`-rpbisect`: each frame fed only its first N strips, N halved by whether the events come, 72 frames a step): the event is a matter of quantity, not of one primitive.** Of the frame's 935 strips: 467 fed, 0 events in 64 frames (both runs); 584, 2 and 2; 613, 0; 627 and 634, 4 and 0; 642, 33 (run 309); 701, 61 and 63; 935, 64 and 64. So bit 9 starts between about 600 and 700 strips a frame here and comes every frame above that. **Not the parameter memory:** the scenes took 35 to 82 pages at every step (the whole frame 70 and 72); the "1,256 to 1,277 pages" in runs 303-308's reports is the cursor read out of order (a distance of about -14 round the ring), a fault of the measure (to fix: both cursor reads under the same chain), and my reading of it as the pool running out was wrong. What rises with the strips: the TA's own time and the chain's length (three descriptors a strip). Open: which quantity (strips, descriptors, the TA's time against the render in flight or the frame's period, primitives in one tile); next: the search's steps with the frame's TA time and the render's state logged beside the events, and the same frame fed with its small quads joined into fewer strips (one strip a line of text) to tell count from area. Decals as polygons are off by default again (`-rpdecalpoly` turns them on): run 305's 14 stalls against run 297's 6  **Run 310 (2026-11-21, the search with the TA's time): the cause of bit 9 measured.** Events against the TA's time a frame, a render in flight at every arm: 7.0 ms (467 strips) 0 of 64; 7.5 ms 0; 7.7 ms 4 to 9; 7.9 ms 18; 8.3 ms 42; 8.8 ms 60; 10.1 ms (all 935) 64; the text's 468 strips alone (6.8 ms) 0, the menu's first 300 (6.1 ms) 0. **Bit 9 is a render ending under a running TA** (the render there ~7.7 ms); runs 302 (18 ms renders, 7 ms TAs: 4 events) and 303 (the reverse: one a frame) agree. **The rule built (b8133e1a): at a frame's arm, a render in flight that would end before the TA does is waited for first; one that will outlast the TA is left beside it. Run 311 (the same console test, 3 min): 10 events (runs 303 and 306: ~8,000 and 12,481), 0 stalls;** 8,481 frames' TA waited (6.1 ms each on average: this test conflicts in every frame; 49 fps against 74), 102 armed beside a render. Next: the owner's 10-minute play with it (decal polygons off): the stalls and the events against run 297's 6 and 1,329 and run 305's 14 and 4,440, the fps against 45.7 (the waits' cost in a game, where few frames conflict); then the decal polygons back on under it  **Run 312 (2026-11-21, the owner's 10-minute play with the rule, b8133e1a, decal polygons off): 0 stalls, 0 rebuilds, 49 bit 9 events** (run 297: 6 stalls and 1,329 events; run 305: 14 and 4,440): the first played run on record with the overlap on and no stall. **40.0 fps over 23,750 frames** (run 297: 45.7; run 275: 44.9): the rule's cost: the TA waited for the render in 16,524 frames (75.4 s in all, 4.6 ms each, 12.7% of the run), armed beside one in 2,718. The wait is made in the frame call, so the game's CPU stands still for it. Next (the fps back without the stall): the arm put off instead of the CPU held: the frame's chain built at the call and armed by the 1 ms pump the moment the render ends, the call returning at once; and the estimate's margin measured (how many of the 16,524 would in fact have ended under the TA). One clean run is one run: the stop rule for calling the stall cured is three played runs of 10 minutes with none  **The owner on run 312 (2026-11-21): "i didn't see any checkerboxes and a lot less texture glitches, but still a couple. much better overall."**  **The wait put off (b2419c69, 2026-11-21 evening):** the frame's chain is built at the call and armed by the 1 ms pump the moment the render in flight ends (`Chain::prepare` / `Chain::arm`, `arm_waiting` in `ports/runner/src/gfx.rs`; the next frame call arms it first if no pump did; `-rpnodefer`: the wait in the call as run 312 had it). The report line now also judges the margin: each put-off frame, once its own TA is done, counted as needed, within 1 ms, or needless. Checked in the emulator only as far as it goes (the walk ends by itself, 0 frames refused): the emulator's chains never finish, so the overlap and the put-off arm never run there. Next: run 313 hands-free (`-rpmenu -rpmenutext`, 3 minutes: bit 9 events against run 311's 10; that test's renders and TAs are alike, so the chip is its limit and its fps may stay near run 311's 49), then the owner's 10-minute play (the fps against run 312's 40.0)  **Run 313 (2026-11-21 evening, hands-free on the X51v, `+rp_emit 2 -rpmenu -rpmenutext`, 3 minutes, `local/half-life/run313`):** the put-off arm works on the chip: 0 stalls, 0 frames refused, 0 TA or render hangs; bit 9 events 3 (run 311: 10); 53.3 fps steady (run 311: 49.4, the same test with the wait inside the frame call). 9,097 frames' TA were put off (82,018 ms in all, 9.0 ms each), every one armed by the pump, none inside the next frame call; 152 armed beside a render. The margin's judge could not settle it in this test: 9,035 of the waits were within 1 ms of the frame's own TA time (59 needed, 3 needless): here a render (9.9 ms) and a TA (8.8 ms) are alike, so the rule runs them one after the other and the chip is the limit. Play is where the CPU is the limit: next the owner's 10-minute play (the fps against run 312's 40.0)  **Run 314 (2026-11-21 evening, the owner's 10-minute play with the put-off arm, `local/half-life/run314`): faster, and the stalls back.** 50.4 fps over 24,750 frames (run 312: 40.0, run 297: 45.7, run 275: 44.9), but 871 bit 9 events (run 312: 49) and 2 stalls, each cured by the full reset (245 ms); the owner: "checkerboard is back", the texture flashing with models stretched out, hitches. The cause read from the counters: with the program let go at once, its next frame call comes while the frame before's TA still runs or its render has just begun, so 18,387 frames (run 312: 2,718) were armed beside a render on the estimate that it would outlast the TA, and only 4,606 waited (run 312: 16,524). The estimate is the last frame's render and TA times, a guess: 871 events in 18,387 such frames (run 312: 49 in 2,718), in bursts (1 event in the run's last 7,000 frames, stood still). The put-off frames' own count: 2,048 needed, 562 within 1 ms, 1,996 needless (4,344 ms in all). **Built, not yet on the X51v:** strict order: no TA is armed while a render runs (every such frame put off to the pump; `-rpbeside`: run 314's way), so no render can end under one; the chip's two stages then run one after the other while the CPU makes the next frame, and the frame rate is bounded by a TA plus a render (about 7 + 13 to 15 ms here: 45 to 50 fps, a sum, not a measurement). The owner's words that the flashing and the stretching come together fit a frame the chip spoiled at an event (run 312: 49 events, "far fewer glitches"); the next play says whether they go with the events  **Run 315 (2026-11-21 evening, strict order 3021f245, hands-free `+rp_emit 2 -rpwalk`, 3 minutes, `local/half-life/run315`):** 0 frames armed beside a render, 1 bit 9 event (run 300, the same walk under the plain overlap: 1,932; run 304: 2), 0 stalls, 0 hangs, 0 refused; 813 of 4,554 frames met a running render and were put off (10 armed inside the next frame call). The walk logs every frame, so it is CPU-bound and no measure of play (25.4 fps; run 304's walk 20.7): the events in play are still to be measured (the owner's next play). The run faulted after its play, at the end shot (R356), so it has no profile  **Run 316 (2026-11-21 night, the owner's 10-minute play, strict order with every fix of that evening, `local/half-life/run316`):** 0 stalls, 1 bit 9 event, 0 resets, no fault at the end; about 40.8 fps (the mean of the 5 s windows; run 314: 49.3 by the same measure, run 312: 40.0, run 297: 45.7, run 275: 44.9): 10,571 of 17,413 put-off frames were armed inside the next frame call (34.5 s, 7% of the run): the chip's two stages one after the other are the limit there.
