The agreed picture behind a menu opened in a game cost that run half of its 1,202 texture rebuilds (its 640 x 480 takes a twelfth of the 2700G's texture memory while the menu is up); now the paused game, dimmed, is behind the in-game menu (as Half-Life shows it; `ui_renderworld` kept on by ref_rp) and the picture is the menu's own when no game runs; seen in the emulator (`-rpmenuhold`). The menu's first-use cost (1.8 s: its fonts and pictures) is paid at the map's start (`-rpnomenuwarm`: not). The 0.37 to 0.40 s frames at each menu opening and closing were card writes (the samples: rename, write, 35 to 42 samples of ~390 ms), the test's own logging (`-rptaplog`, `-rppenlog`) the likely writer: both left out of the play kit  **Run 317:** the owner refused the paused game behind the in-game menu ("i told you to make menu look like this, like the real game", his picture of the agreed screen) and gave the way: the picture only while the menu is up, cleared when it closes. Built (3ec15951, his idea taken one step on): the picture is written into the first two pages of the map's own texture room, idle while a menu covers the screen, their bytes copied to the program's memory first and copied back at the first face of the world drawn again (`RP_BorrowPicture`, `RP_BorrowReturn`; `-rpnoborrow`: the old tiles): nothing of the map's pushed out or rebuilt, video memory held only while a menu is up, 1 MiB of RAM for that time. Seen in the emulator (the agreed picture behind the in-game menu; one borrow and one return in a play run); the chip's picture of it and the two copies' time on the X51v are for the next play. The "Done" pauses (0.37 to 0.40 s) are the settings file written at every page left: now written only when a hash of the saved settings and key bindings has changed  **Memory (2026-11-21 night, the emulator's play run with everything in):** out of memory at the menu's first opening: the saved pages' 1 MiB and the picture decoded whole (0.9 MiB) at the same moment, the heap at 37.6 MiB. Fixed: the picture is written into the borrowed pages a band of eight rows at a time from its 8-bit form and its colours (`R_TexCompact`), never decoded whole; the heap then peaks at 36 MiB with the menu up (of about 37.6: a thin margin, said to the owner). If the megabyte cannot be had later in a play, the menu's words are drawn on a plain ground for that frame and it is asked for again (never the old tiles from there: their decode would stop the engine)  **Corrected and closed further (2026-11-22, the owner: "close all opened things that need fixed"):** the out of memory above was not the menu picture: the switch chosen for the new self-checks, `-rptexcheck`, was already R335's own check, which decodes every texture whole as it loads; at the menu it decoded eight pictures at once (the background's four levels 1,996 KiB, six logo pictures 534 to 1,063 KiB each: the `rp-tex: a large decode` lines now say so), and both failing runs had it on. Every run without it passed. The self-checks are `-rpplacecheck` now. The two changes made for it stand as savings: the picture is written in bands from its 8-bit form, and it now takes the texture room's LAST two pages, which the map has not reached (the room is filled from its start; 2,157 of 3,072 KiB in run 317): only the bytes the map really has there are saved, 0 KiB in the emulator's play run where the first two pages had cost 1 MiB. | R352 | **Some textures flash and glitch** (the owner, run 293, 2026-11-20). Not read yet. Leads: kit 113's overlap lets the CPU write the next frame's textures and lightmaps (R335's ring rebuilds, R297 B's lightmap pages) while an older render still reads the same pages (ref_rp waits for the render only when its pool is full, `RP_LmFrame`), a write-after-read hazard the serial path never had; or the R344 rebuilds' texture placing. Next: count pages written while a render that used them is in flight (a generation per page against the render's frame), and the A/B overlap on/off at one spot  **The owner's photos (run 293):** one face's patch on a ledge (a small rectangle where two walls meet) goes bright and back, frame to frame, the rest steady. Run 293 wrote 0 lightmaps and 0 textures a frame there (R297 B, R335), so not a write hazard at that moment; next suspect: the face's second pass (texture then lightmap, R297 B) fighting on depth when its corners land a step apart (a near-equal z), or a face drawn alone in one frame and both ways in the next. Run 293: 4 stalls in 10 min (frames 744, 895, 12,613, 12,881), each cured by the rebuild; 37.4 fps over 22,250 frames (run 287: 45.4)  **Run 295:** 0 faces changed drawing way between frames (the counter, 5f42b9cf's successor): the flash is not option B against the lit surface; ~850 face draws a report went the lit-surface way for a decal (steady, not flipping). Next suspects: two coplanar faces (or a face and its decal surface) fighting on depth, the second pass's z against the first's on a face whose corners round differently, a texture page rewritten while drawn  **Run 297, the owner:** the flashing, the hitching and the checkerboards come especially with the flashlight on (see R340's run 297 note: dynamic-light lightmap rewrites into pages a render in flight reads)  **Session f71b5a8a (2026-11-21):** the hold lever built for R340 (`-rptexhold S`: no lightmap or texture written while the TA and a render are both at work) also keeps a render from reading a lightmap half rewritten in place (`RP_LmPlace` writes a stale face's lightmap over its old place while the frame before may still be drawing it); run 298's two halves tell whether the flashing follows the hold (the owner's eye: flashing in the first 5 minutes against the last 5)  **One hazard found in the code and fixed (not yet on the X51v):** a texture page was dropped and given to other faces if it was last drawn from two frames ago, though with kit 113's overlap that frame's render may still be reading it (the rule waited only for a page drawn from in the last frame: written for one frame in flight); `RP_IN_FLIGHT` 2 now, the waits counted. Rare by the logs (run 297: 27 pages dropped in 10 minutes, run 293: 3), so it is not the whole of what the owner sees. Still open: a lit face's lightmap is rewritten in place every frame under a dynamic light (run 297: up to 8 a frame, 7 for the flashlight) while the two frames in flight read it; the owner's words (2026-11-21): "it kinda reminds me of what the throwable weapon models were doing", asked which it is (a wrong picture or brightness for a moment, or a surface stretched into bars), not answered yet  **A cause proven from run 297's own log (session f71b5a8a, 2026-11-21): faces with a decal are drawn the other way.** `RP_ChipFaceLmTry` sends any face that has a decal (a bullet hole, blood, a scorch: `fa->pdecals`) down the lit-surface path instead of option B, because only ref_soft's surface cache can bake a decal in; the two paths do not look alike (option B's light is coloured and multiplied by the chip, the lit surface's is ref_soft's grey levels), so the face's whole rectangle changes look the frame a decal lands on it and changes back when the decal is retired. Run 297: 35 faces changed way between two frames in a row (21 and 14 in two reports: each a flash), and in 13 of its 20 reports decal faces were drawn the other way (12 to 867 face draws a report); run 295's 0 was a run with no shooting. It fits the owner's photos (one face's patch on a ledge bright and back) and "worst in fire fights". **The fix (rule 0; not built):** GL draws a decal as its own blended polygon over the face (ref_gl's `DrawDecalsBatch`); ref_soft bakes it into the face's cached surface; ours keeps the face on option B always and draws each decal as a see-through polygon after it (`R_DecalSetupVerts` gives the clipped corners; the decal's texture written once as ARGB4444 from its palette's alpha ramp; blended, depth compared and not written, as the models' blends), so no face ever changes way and a decal costs a few corners a frame instead of a surface rebuild. Check in the emulator first (a wall shot in the scripted fight, the picture before and after), then the X51v  **Built and checked in the emulator (session f71b5a8a, 2026-11-21; not on the X51v: R353):** a decal face now stays on option B and each decal is drawn over it as a blended fan (`RP_FaceDecals`, `RP_DecalPoly` in `r_emit.c`: the face's corners clipped to the decal's picture here, since the engine's `R_DecalSetupVerts` needs the GL renderer's per-face polygons and hung the first try; the effects' ARGB4444 words; the decal's depth nudged 2^-9 toward the eye). The scripted walk on c2a5f (`-rpwalk -rpquiet`, the map's own painted decals): 354 to 1,015 decals drawn a report, 0 faces left option B for a decal, **0 faces changed drawing way between frames (18 to 21 a report before, in the same walk)**, 0 frames refused, the run ended by itself. A correction to the note above: the 21 changes in run 297's first report are this same start of the map (its painted decals), so they were decal faces too. Not seen by eye yet (the emulator's end picture does not show the game's frame): on the X51v check that decals sit on their walls without speckle (the depth nudge), are not too bright in dark rooms (they are unlit, as ref_soft's), and that brush entities' decals (doors) hold. `-rpdecalsoft` is the old way for a side by side. Later: decals lit by their face's lightmap as GL does; the polygon kept per decal instead of clipped every frame  **Run 305 (the owner's play with the decal polygons; his video):** the alcove's wall and floor at the start spot drawn for a frame or two with other pictures, back and forth, the flashlight on; 14 stalls. Whether the polygons themselves draw wrongly (a decal's clipped fan with the atlas page's other pictures) or the frame's extra small blended primitives bring R340's fault on is not known; the end shot of the same view (drawn with the chip waited for) is clean. **The polygons are off by default again** (`rp_decal_on` 0, `-rpdecalpoly` on): the old way flashes when a decal lands but adds nothing small to the frame. Next: the start spot's view in the emulator with `-rpdecalpoly`, its picture read back (the device's end shot path: `frame-gpu.565`) with and without, before the polygons go back on  **The owner on run 312 (the hold rule, decal polygons off): "a lot less texture glitches, but still a couple"**: so most of what he saw went with R340's fault (a render ending under a running TA), and a couple remain to find (the old way's flash when a decal lands is one known source)  **The owner on run 314 (2026-11-21 evening):** "the texture starts to flash and the model get all stretched out", "like the bone issue": the two come together, with hitches. That fits a whole frame spoiled by the chip (R340: 871 bit 9 events in run 314 against run 312's 49, where he saw "far fewer glitches") better than a picture written at the wrong moment; R340's strict order (built, not yet run) is the test. Check with `-rpdecalshot` in the emulator's software picture and one hands-free fight on the X51v, the last frame looked at  **Runs 321-323 (2026-11-22, hands-free on the X51v):** 321 and 322, the camera still at the fight's place, polygons off and on: the chip's last frames the same, no patch. 323, the fight with the polygons on and large ones logged: the red trapezoid is again in the last frame, and the only large decal polygon of the run is the map's own `{bigblood1` (96 x 96, with alpha, flags 0x3) on face 121, about 30 x 266 TA pixels, from frame 1; 0 triangles drawn plainly (its picture is placed). The polygons stay off (`-rpdecalpoly`); the play kit is without them   **Runs 324-327 (2026-11-22, hands-free on the X51v; the owner: "i want the bullet and blood decals to work"): solved.** (1) The red trapezoid of runs 320, 323 and 324 is **Half-Life's own damage-direction arrow** (the HUD's red fading wedge beside the crosshair when the god-moded player is shot): it sits at the same screen place in views pitched 10 and 50 degrees, is drawn over the gun, and run 327 shows it top-centre in ordinary frames 502 and 612 and right of centre in the end frame. Not a decal. (2) The engine's bug that kept bullet holes from staying: ref_soft's `R_DecalVertsClip` reads the GL renderer's per-face polygon, which the software renderer never builds, and returned without setting the count, so `R_DecalCreate` kept or dropped each new decal on a stack word's leftover. Fixed in `xash3d-fwgs.patch` (the corners from the map's edges into Xash's own clip, b98784be); run 327 drew 347 and 231 decal polygons a report (run 325: 104 and 8). (3) Decal triangles across the near plane now clipped in fixed point at the face's own plane (they went to ref_soft's float clip with a stale matrix; 75fa466a). (4) Run 326/327 pictures: a bullet hole drawn as a polygon on the floor, see-through edges, in place. **The polygons are on by default** (`-rpdecalsoft` the old way); `-rpshots`/`-rpfloorshot` take the chip's picture of ordinary frames. | R355 | **Looking around with the stylus is jerky** (the owner, run 312, 2026-11-21: "the mouse look with the stylus is not smooth at all and very jerky when looking around"). Not read yet. Leads: how often the touch chip is read (`ports/sys/src/input.rs`: the pad's interval) against the frame rate, so a frame gets a move or none; the stylus's raw samples unfiltered (the panel's noise as view jitter); the move applied whole in the frame it arrives (`Platform_MouseMove` in `in_rp.c`: the sum since the last call) with no smoothing across frames. Rule 0: Windows Mobile hands a game raw pen points at the driver's rate; ours can time-stamp each sample and spread the turn evenly over the frames between samples (and filter the panel's noise by its measured size). Measure first: the samples a second and their jitter with the stylus held still and dragged steadily, logged by a run  **Read and a measure built (7b1449a4, 2026-11-21 evening), nothing changed yet:** by reading (not measured): the pad is read only when the game reads its clock or polls, at most every 4 ms (`READ_EVERY_US`), so the readings follow the game's frames, not a clock of their own; the OS's touch filter (`drivers/touch-cal`, tuned for taps) drops a reading whenever the pressure is not steady and then wants two that agree; the positions reach the game in whole screen pixels (about 7 raw units each). `-rppenlog` logs each stroke when it lifts: the readings a second, the gaps between them, the filter's answers, the raw step between readings and the raw range (a stylus held still shows the panel's noise), and on the game's side the frames with no move and the largest move in a frame. To run in the owner's next play: the stylus held still 5 s, then one slow steady drag, then play  **Run 314 (`-rppenlog`, 44 strokes, 20.9 s under the stylus):** the pad gives 191 readings a second (most under 6 ms apart), so the readings are not the shortage; the filter passed 2,283 as moves, held 1,377 (35%) and dropped 286 (7%); the game drew 732 frames in those strokes and 76 (10%) got no move at all, the largest move in one frame 162 pixels; the raw step between two readings was over 50 units (7 pixels) for 15% of stroke 1's readings. The owner (same run): "the mouse look still isnt very smooth" (nothing was changed in it). The run had hitches of its own (R340: 2 resets, frames up to 307 ms), so the look is to be judged again once those are gone; the design (rule 0, to check with the owner): the filter's holds and the frames with no move are the two measured leads. The 5-second hold and the slow drag asked for were not in the log (only the first 40 strokes and the long ones are kept)  **Built (2026-11-21 night; tests on the PC, a scripted drag in the emulator; not yet on the X51v):** the stylus's path steadied in `ports/sys/src/input.rs` (`Smooth`): every raw reading the touch filter counts while the pen is down (its moves and its holds) goes through a median of three and a 1-euro filter in integers on the pad's units (cut-off 2.5 Hz at rest, rising 0.015 Hz for each raw unit a second; the time between readings measured), handed on in steps of five eighths of a pixel so a resting stylus cannot flicker between two pixels; a move is said whenever the place reaches another pixel. `-rpnopensmooth`: run 314's way. Guard test: a noisy slow drag arrives as steps of one pixel, never back, and a stylus held still under the same noise says nothing. The emulator's 120-pixel drag reached the game as 88 moves. The cut-offs are a first fit to run 314's numbers: the owner's eye and the next run's stroke lines judge them  **Run 316's cause, measured (2026-11-21 night, that run's samples frame by frame, `RETROPANE_PROFILE_FRAMES`):** eight of the forty slowest frames of play (101 to 245 ms) were 49% to 66% inside `rp_input_poll`: the game drains its events in a loop, a poll that found the queue empty read the pad again (a millisecond with the stylus down), and a moving stylus gave each reading a new event, so the loop went round while the stylus moved (run 314 had it too: 892 samples of its slow frames). Fixed: the pad is read once an interval (4 ms), by the poll or the clock's pump, never once a poll; guard test `a_drain_loop_under_a_moving_stylus_reads_the_pad_once`. With the steadied path (which says a move nearly every reading) this was the look's own freeze. The owner's eye on the next play | the owner, 2026-11-21 | after:R340's arm put off | done run 318 (the owner: the stylus look smooth) |

The fps gain is for the owner's play (the emulator's frame time is the PC's)  **Run 330 (2026-11-22, the owner's 10-minute play with it): 30.6 fps average (run 318: 35.0), no fault, no chip reset, returned clean.** Not a slower kit: run 330 stayed about three times as long in the late alien-grunt section (20-27 fps in both runs) and less in the calm middle (40-55 in both); place by place the windows match (the start 37-39 against 34-36, the late fight 18.6-26.0 against 11.9-26.5). But the integer trace did not pay: in the slow frames the float helpers it removed went (`rp_fdot3_asm` from the trace 1.6% to 0.4%, the compares' `rp_fcmpgt_asm` 0.8% to 0) and its own integer work took their place (`PM_RecursiveHullCheck` itself 5.0% to 6.7%, `RP_TraceContents` 3.1% to 4.7%, `RP_TracePoint` 0.8% to 1.6%, `RP_PlaneFix` 0.5%): the traces about 24% of those frames before and 25% after. Leads (rule 0, not built): each recursive call converts both its ends again (`RP_TracePoint` twice an entry, once more for each crossing's point test) where the child's ends are its parent's mid, which could be handed down in 16.16; the plane converted again at each visit (a small cache keyed by the plane's float bits, or kit 109's cooked table extended to the clip hulls at the map's cook); `SV_WaterLinks` 1.6% to 2.3% (more water brushes in the later area). Stays open  **Next step built after run 330 (a68545bb, not yet on the X51v):** the crossing's mid converted once and handed down (RP_HullCheckQ), planes from a 256-entry cache checked by their float bits (RP_PlaneGet); -rphullcheck 0 differences again (fight ~24,600 traces, walk ~67,600). Its cost is the next play's profile against run 330's slow frames (traces ~25%)  **Run 331 (2026-11-22, the owner's play with a68545bb): 30.8 fps over 18,250 frames, returned clean, no reset** (run 330: 30.6, run 318: 35.0; the same long late section at 15.6-25 fps). In the slow frames the traces are 27.6% (run 330: 27.9%): `RP_TracePoint` halved (1.6% to 0.9%), `RP_PlaneGet` 0.4%, but the node walk itself stays (`RP_HullCheckQ` 6.8%, `RP_TraceContents` 4.3%) with `SV_ClipToEntity` 4.7% and `SV_WaterLinks` 2.4%: the cost is now the number of traces and entities tested (the alien grunts' and hornets' moves, the monsters' trial walks: 98 for one headcrab's chase start in run 330), not the arithmetic of each. Next lever is fewer traces with the same answers (rule 0), not faster ones | Claude, 2026-11-22 | session | open |

Run 333 showed only that switching it to 1.0e-9 changes nothing at that one view (not the wall-top flashing's cause); what the word does is still unmeasured. The plays differ by what the player does: with the flashlight on (`-rpflash`, run 337a) 18,187 ms (27.4 fps, +3.2 ms a frame), and of that **the held rewrites' wait is 1.3 ms** (run 337b, `-rpnostage`: 17,541 ms, 28.5 fps; second half 31.3 against 33.1 fps), the walls' relighting on the CPU the rest. **Next (a fresh session):** (1) rewrites never written where an in-flight frame reads and never waited for: a face whose place was drawn in the last two frames is relit into a three-slot ring in video memory, else written in place (no wait, no copy; the wall's plain lightmap stays home when the light leaves); (2) the flashlight and other dynamic lights drawn by the 2700G as a second additive pass over the faces they touch (texture x light added: the same sum as relighting the lightmap, no CPU relight, no upload), measured with `-rpflash` against run 337a; then `porting-lessons.md` updated with the result  **Runs 338a-b (2026-11-22, the same `-rpfight -rpflash`, one build):** fix 1 built (the lightmap ring, r_emit.c `RP_RingAlloc`): **17,539 ms, 28.5 fps** (second half 33.1), against the old hold in the same kit (`-rplmhold`, 338b) 18,188 ms, 27.4 fps, the same as run 337a: the whole 1.3 ms back (= run 337b's `-rpnostage`, without its hazard); 2,745 lightmaps went to the ring, 0 held, 0 without room; no hangs.
